What you will learn

How the client record is built across CRM, KYC, data room, reporting and the answer library; why approved channels matter legally; and a disciplined way to use generative AI on client documents without losing accuracy or confidentiality.

Where you meet it at KKR

Salesforce and CRM notes [S103]; KYC and reporting duties in the London analyst role [S101]; the AI requirement in the same posting [S101]; KKR's central AI platform hiring [S443] CONFIRMED.

Only at KKR

Honestly, the tools themselves (Salesforce, data rooms) are industry standard. What is specific is the combination: a firm building a "centralized AI platform" across the business [S443] while its client team handles DDQs whose facts can be checked against public SEC filings [S420, S425]. That is a good setting in which to learn AI drafting with verification, inferred from the sources INFERRED.

Say it in one line

"I want to learn how KCS keeps a complete client record and uses KKR's approved AI tools on documents like DDQs, with every fact still traced to a source."

A worked week-three task: five DDQ answers on GII V@@l5-week3@@

The brief from your relationship manager (illustrative)WEEK 3

Setup A Nordic public pension (hypothetical) has sent five questions on Global Infrastructure Investors V (GII V). Answers are due Friday. KKR covers the Nordics from Stockholm and Copenhagen (Carl Lithander, Victor Kihlgren, Adam Bjorndal, Fanny Jorwall) [S155] CONFIRMED.

The first catch GII V held its final close on 3 August 2026 [S430] CONFIRMED, so a new commitment to the main fund is no longer possible. The realistic versions of this task are an existing investor's annual monitoring DDQ, or a client weighing co-investment alongside GII V. Spotting this before drafting is the first test INFERRED.

The rule Use only sourced public facts. Mark every point that needs the deal team, Finance or Legal. Never estimate a performance number.

Q1. Describe the fund: size, strategy, geography. (ILPA section 2)PUBLIC FACTS

Draft "GII V is a $19.2 billion Core+ fund focused on investing in critical infrastructure assets and businesses primarily in North America and Western Europe. It is the fifth vintage of the strategy and KKR's largest infrastructure fund raised to date."

Source log KKR press release, 3 Aug 2026 [S430] CONFIRMED. Quote the release wording; do not paraphrase "Core+" into a risk claim.

Needs an owner Investment period dates and fee terms come from the fund documents: Legal and the infrastructure product team.

Q2. Which investments has the fund made to date? (ILPA section 4 and Appendix F)PARTLY PUBLIC

Draft "At final close GII V had committed over $9 billion, including investments in Altitude III, EDF power solutions North America, Enilive, FiberCop, Global Technical Realty, Gulf Data Hub, Metronet, Sempra Infrastructure and The Parking Spot."

Source log [S430] CONFIRMED. Date the statement "as of 3 August 2026".

Needs the deal team Commitment per asset, current valuation, and anything signed since August. Appendix F asks for portfolio data in a table; valuation marks are confidential and come from Finance INFERRED.

Q3. Describe the team and platform. (ILPA section 9)PUBLIC FACTS

Draft "KKR's infrastructure platform manages approximately $120 billion of infrastructure equity, up from $13 billion in 2019, across more than 100 investments, with approximately 160 dedicated infrastructure executives. KKR has committed over $70 billion of equity across the Global Infrastructure strategy in North America and Europe."

Source log [S430] CONFIRMED. In EMEA, the infrastructure strategy is co-led from London by Tara Davies, Co-Head of KKR EMEA and Co-Head of European Infrastructure [S158] CONFIRMED.

Needs an owner Named key persons, departures and hires (section 3 and Appendix B): product team with HR and Legal, because key-person terms are contractual INFERRED.

Q4. Do you offer co-investment, and on what terms? (ILPA section 5)NEEDS KCM AND LEGAL

Draft (firm-level only) "KKR Capital Markets provides syndication services for co-investments in transactions in which KKR and its vehicles participate. Since 2014, KKR's fund agreements typically require 100% of monitoring, transaction and break-up fees allocable to a fund to be shared with fund investors."

Source log KKR 10-K FY2025 [S425] CONFIRMED. GII IV comparison: closed at $17bn in March 2022 [S431] [Reported: search snippet, verify before use].

Needs KCM and Legal ILPA's actual questions: fees, management fee or carry charged to co-investors in predecessor funds; whether co-investments were syndicated; any co-underwriting before signing; treatment of broken-deal expenses [S444] CONFIRMED. None of these GII-specific answers is public.

The trap Do not mention any live co-investment by name: the client is not wall-crossed (Figure 4). If the pension wants co-investment, the RM logs the interest in the CRM and KCM decides on approach INFERRED.

Q5. Provide the track record of predecessor funds. (ILPA section 14)FINANCE ONLY

Draft "Track record for Global Infrastructure Investors I to IV is provided in the attached performance schedule, prepared by KKR's finance team as of [date], showing gross and net IRR and multiple." The schedule itself is left blank for Finance.

Source log Public: fund sizes only (GII IV $17bn; GII III $7.2bn [S431] REPORTED). No predecessor performance is in our public sources [Gap].

Needs Finance and Compliance Gross and net figures, and for newer funds the effect of subscription lines, which the 2025 ILPA Performance Template standardises [S445] CONFIRMED. Compliance checks presentation (see the rules section).

Why it works The junior adds value by building the shell, the source log and the owner list, not by guessing numbers INFERRED.

What a Nordic pension will probably also ask

Nordic pensions are often strong on responsible investment, so section 19 (ESG, per the PRI questionnaire) is likely in a real version INFERRED. No GII V-specific ESG data is in our sources [Gap]: route it to the sustainability team rather than drafting from general KKR statements.

What you will learn

How to turn a client's questions into an ILPA-mapped plan, draft only what public sources support, build the source log as you go, and hand the right people the right gaps early in the week.

Where you meet it at KKR

GII V's investor list spans "pension plans, sovereign wealth funds, insurance companies, asset managers, private wealth platforms, and family offices" [S430] CONFIRMED. The biggest dedicated KCS product cluster in London is infrastructure (Rory Smith, James Hewitt, Larisa Svintsitski, Pablo Arribas) [S150] CONFIRMED.

Only at KKR

The same infrastructure team's ideas reach clients through GII V, the open-ended Diversified Core Infrastructure Fund, K-INFRA for individuals, and co-investment [S421, S430, S432] CONFIRMED. So the five answers above would read differently for each wrapper, and a junior learns to keep one fact base consistent across all of them INFERRED.

Say it in one line

"I want to learn to answer a pension's DDQ on a fund like GII V from primary sources, knowing exactly which answers I can write and which only the deal team or Finance can give."

@@l6@@
Part 6

The news, turned into lessons

Twelve stories from April to October 2026 that KKR drove or that hit its client business. For each: what happened, the technical concept it teaches, where a KCS intern would meet it, and one line you can use.

How to read this part@@l6-how@@

Commercial awareness usually means knowing the headline. This part goes one step further: every story is a worked example of a mechanism you would handle in the KCS seat. Figure 1 maps stories to skills. Figure 2 puts them on one timeline. Then each story gets the same block: what happened (dated and sourced), what you will learn (the concept, with numbers), where you meet it at KKR, and one line to use. Each section ends with an "Only at KKR" box.

Fundraising: two flagship closes@@l6-closes@@

Story 1. North America Fund XIV (about $23bn) and Global Infrastructure Investors V ($19.2bn)

What happened. On 2 April 2026 KKR closed North America Fund XIV ("NAX4") at about $23bn, which it calls the largest private equity fund focused only on North America [S335] CONFIRMED. The release reported a 23% gross IRR (19% net) across the three predecessor funds as of 31 December 2025 [S335] REPORTED. KKR's Q2 vehicle table shows $21.9bn committed and an investment period from April 2025 to April 2031 [S1] CONFIRMED. On 3 August 2026 KKR closed Global Infrastructure Investors V at $19.2bn, its largest infrastructure fund, a Core+ fund focused on North America and Western Europe, with over $9bn already committed to deals and investors including "pension plans, sovereign wealth funds, insurance companies, asset managers, private wealth platforms, and family offices" [S334] CONFIRMED. Brandon Donnenfeld credited "longstanding clients who have partnered with us across multiple fund vintages and new investors from around the world" [S334] CONFIRMED.

What you will learn

Close mechanics and fee switch-on. A fund holds a first close, later closes and a final close; investors who join late typically pay their share of earlier calls (equalisation) INFERRED. In private equity, fees usually run on committed capital in the investment period (1.0% to 2.0%) [S2] CONFIRMED. At 30 June 2026 KKR had $72bn of committed capital not yet paying fees, at an average of about 90 basis points [S1] CONFIRMED. Worked: $72bn × 0.9% ≈ $650m a year of management fees that switch on as money is invested INFERRED. GII V had over $9bn of $19.2bn committed at close: 9 ÷ 19.2 ≈ 47% INFERRED.

Where you meet it at KKR

"Partnering with clients to help with KYC & investment onboarding" and "Supporting Relationship Managers in fundraising activities" [S101] CONFIRMED. A re-up decision at a pension like CalPERS, which committed $600m to North America Fund XIII [S117] CONFIRMED, is exactly the decision a NAX4 close depends on INFERRED.

Say it in one line

"I want to learn what happens between a first and a final close, how commitments become called capital and how fees switch on, using a live fund like GII V."

Only at KKR

GII V's investor list puts private wealth platforms beside sovereigns [S334], and in the same quarter KKR's real assets money also came from K-Series Infrastructure and Helix [S74] CONFIRMED. One team raises the same asset class through drawdown, evergreen and platform routes, so a junior sees how one strategy is explained to three audiences INFERRED.

Wealth and liquidity@@l6-wealth@@

Story 2. K-Series grows to $42bn, and its fees change shape

What happened. K-Series AUM grew from $6bn (2023) to $16bn (2024) and $34bn (2025) [S72] CONFIRMED, then $38bn at 31 March 2026 [S331] and $42bn at 30 June 2026 [S73] CONFIRMED. About 85% sits in private equity and infrastructure [S76] REPORTED. Scott Nuttall: "we're up 20% net year-to-date" [S76] REPORTED. From Q2 2026, about $160m of K-Series private equity performance revenue is reported as fee-related performance revenue [S73] CONFIRMED, carrying a 15% to 20% compensation rate rather than the 70% to 80% used for carried interest [S1] CONFIRMED.

What you will learn

Evergreen arithmetic. Change in NAV = subscriptions − redemptions + investment return. Fees run on NAV (1.25% for K-PEC main classes [S111] CONFIRMED). Performance fees on evergreen vehicles are paid above a preferred return and a high-water mark [S1] CONFIRMED. Worked high-water mark (illustrative): NAV per share 100 → 110 (fee earned on the gain above the hurdle), falls to 95, recovers to 105: no new performance fee, because 105 is still below the 110 peak.

Where you meet it at KKR

Monthly and quarterly materials for private banks; the EMEA Wealth Communications team in London [S102] CONFIRMED; adviser education at "KKR Academies, having 100 advisers at a time come in" [S207] CONFIRMED.

Say it in one line

"I want to understand evergreen funds like K-PRIME from the inside: how NAV, fees and flows interact, and why KKR now reports their performance fees as recurring earnings."

Story 3. K-FIT pro-rates redemptions, inside a $20.8bn industry wave

What happened. In Q1 2026 investors in KKR FS Income Trust (K-FIT, a non-traded private credit BDC) "tried to redeem 6.3% of shares", so the fund paid about 80% of each request; in Q2 requests fell to 1.65% and were paid in full (Bloomberg, 6 July 2026) [S338] REPORTED. Industry-wide, Q1 requests to withdraw from private credit funds reached $20.8bn; managers with about $300bn of assets paid "just over half"; Blackstone and Oaktree paid above the 5% cap while Apollo, Ares, Blue Owl, HPS and Morgan Stanley kept their limits; the trigger was worry about loans to software companies, while "underlying loan performance remains largely stable" [S337] REPORTED. Apollo Debt Solutions received requests of 11.2% and honoured 5%; Blackstone's BCRED paid 100% of 7.9% [S278] REPORTED. Nuttall said software is "about 6% of our AUM" [S362] REPORTED.

What you will learn

Why caps exist and how pro-rating works. A semi-liquid fund holds illiquid loans or companies but promises periodic exits. The cap stops early leavers draining the cash that remaining investors rely on, which also removes the incentive to rush for the door INFERRED. Under AIFMD II, open-ended EU funds must choose at least two liquidity management tools from a list that includes gates, notice periods, redemption fees, swing pricing, redemption in kind and side pockets [S295] CONFIRMED. Contrast: closed-end private debt funds returned cash at a 24.1% distribution rate and behaved "differently from evergreen vehicles" [S260] REPORTED.

Where you meet it at KKR

Drafting investor letters and adviser FAQs, answering private bank questions consistently, and updating the liquidity section of DDQs INFERRED from the duties in [S101, S141]. The London wealth product team runs the evergreen PE vehicles that carry the same kind of terms [S133] CONFIRMED.

Say it in one line

"When K-FIT received 6.3% redemption requests against a 5% cap, it paid about 80% of each; I want to learn to explain that mechanism to a private bank before the queue forms, not after."

Only at KKR

Blackstone also runs several evergreen funds (BREIT, BCRED, BXPE, BXINFRA) [S213] CONFIRMED, so breadth alone is not unique. What a KKR junior sees in 2026 is a liquidity event in one product (K-FIT) handled while the wider K-Series kept net inflows, "up 20% net year-to-date" [S76, S338] REPORTED, with the evergreen private equity product team sitting in London [S133] CONFIRMED. That is a live lesson in containing one product's problem INFERRED. Partners Group shows the downside elsewhere: requests at one evergreen fund reached an estimated 9.8% and its shares fell up to 18.2% in a day [S339] REPORTED.

Distribution and coverage@@l6-distribution@@

Story 4. Capital Group and KKR: public-private interval funds

What happened. The two firms launched Capital Group KKR Core Plus+ and Multi-Sector+ on 29 April 2025: about 60% public fixed income and 40% private credit, quarterly repurchase offers of up to 10% of shares at NAV, 84 basis points total expense ratio for Core Plus+ (F3), with KKR Credit Advisors sub-advising the private sleeve [S92] CONFIRMED. A public-private equity fund followed; KKR's 10-K says its private side "invests in a K-Series private equity vehicle" [S70] CONFIRMED. In December 2025 the partners announced a target-date fund for 401(k) plans and public-private model portfolios [S363] REPORTED. One independent analysis puts the three funds at about $703m combined on 30 June 2026 [S364] REPORTED, a low-authority source. Nuttall: Capital Group has "relationships with 220,000 of the 300,000 advisers in the United States" [S362] REPORTED.

What you will learn

How a liquidity buffer works. Private share after a repurchase = private ÷ (total − redemption). Worked: 40 ÷ (100 − 10) = 44.4%; then 40 ÷ (90 − 9) = 49.4%. Two full redemption quarters push a "40% private" fund near 50% unless new money or sales rebalance it INFERRED. You also learn the sub-adviser role: KKR runs a sleeve inside another manager's product [S92].

Where you meet it at KKR

Daniel Celeghin heads "New Markets and Sales Partnerships", covering defined contribution and the Capital Group and Itau distribution partnerships [S136] CONFIRMED. A London junior meets the same logic when a European partner asks how K-Series could sit inside a model portfolio INFERRED.

Say it in one line

"I want to learn how a 60/40 public-private fund like Capital Group KKR Core Plus+ keeps its promise of 10% quarterly liquidity, and what that does to the private share over time."

Story 5. Arctos closes and KKR Solutions is formed

What happened. KKR agreed on 4 February 2026 to buy Arctos Partners (minority stakes in sports franchises and solutions for other private equity firms) for $1.4bn initial consideration plus up to $550m earn-out [S8] CONFIRMED. It closed on 4 May 2026, adding $16.0bn of AUM, about $20bn by 30 June [S1] CONFIRMED. Arctos forms "KKR Solutions", reported as home to "a scaled multi-asset class secondaries business" [S355] REPORTED. Arctos Keystone Partners Fund I was one of three named drivers of Q2 private equity fundraising [S74] CONFIRMED. CFO Lewin said the chance to "create flow for Global Atlantic" was critical to the case [S207] CONFIRMED.

What you will learn

Secondaries as an LP liquidity tool. An LP-led secondary sells a fund stake early; a GP-led deal moves assets into a continuation vehicle. Evercore counted $121bn of secondary volume in H1 2026 ($65bn GP-led, $56bn LP-led), with good buyout stakes clearing near 90% of NAV [S267] CONFIRMED. Worked: an LP sells a stake with NAV $50m at 90% = $45m cash now, a $5m discount, to fix a denominator problem or fund new commitments INFERRED.

Where you meet it at KKR

Conversations with LPs who are short of distributions: Bain reports distributions below 15% of NAV for four years [S262] CONFIRMED. KKR Solutions' Customized Portfolio Solutions team works on "strategic partnerships and other bespoke private market solutions" [S85] CONFIRMED.

Say it in one line

"With Arctos now in KKR Solutions, I want to learn how secondaries work as a liquidity tool KCS can offer clients who are waiting for distributions."

Story 6. Quilter Cheviot in the UK and Doha Bank in Qatar

What happened. On 10 December 2025 KKR and Quilter Cheviot announced that the UK wealth manager would offer KKR's evergreen private equity strategy to its clients from January 2026; Markus Egloff called it "an important milestone in our efforts to deliver greater access to KKR's strategies" [S177] CONFIRMED. On 21 September 2026 KKR and Doha Bank signed a memorandum of understanding to "explore a strategic partnership" widening access to KKR's private markets strategies for eligible investors in Qatar, signed by David Petraeus, chairman of KKR Middle East [S367] REPORTED.

What you will learn

The anatomy of a distribution partnership. The manufacturer (KKR) designs the product and its target market; the distributor (the wealth manager or bank) owns client suitability and advice. MiFID II, AIFMD and ELTIF rules put suitability and conflict duties on distributors too [S70] CONFIRMED. UK firms must also meet the Consumer Duty, in force for open products since 31 July 2023 [S308] CONFIRMED. An MoU is a step before a binding distribution agreement INFERRED.

Where you meet it at KKR

Global Wealth Solutions International, led from London, with a Head of Northern Europe and the Middle East (Odumade Odutola) [S124, S137] CONFIRMED; Middle East private bank coverage from Dubai [S151] CONFIRMED. The junior builds the onboarding pack, the partner's training material and the due diligence answers INFERRED.

Say it in one line

"I want to learn what a private bank partnership like Quilter Cheviot actually requires: who checks suitability, who owns the client, and what KKR must give the distributor."

Story 7. Bhavit Patel takes over Asia-Pacific client coverage

What happened. On 8 June 2026 Bloomberg reported that KKR appointed New York-based Bhavit Patel to head its Asia-Pacific institutional and family capital franchise, succeeding the retiring Kate Richdale, in a memo from Eric Mogelof [S358] REPORTED. KKR also hired Katrina Wong as a KCS Principal in Hong Kong in April 2026 [S359] REPORTED. KKR says it has "nearly 1,000 people" in Asia [S3] CONFIRMED and its outlook says "We want to own more Asia at this point in the cycle" [S361] CONFIRMED.

What you will learn

The coverage model. KCS splits coverage three ways: by region, by channel (institutional, family capital, wealth) and by product specialist [S141, S150] CONFIRMED. A leadership change tests whether relationships live in the firm's records or in one person's head INFERRED. CRM discipline is a stated junior skill: "meeting/call notes in CRM" [S103] CONFIRMED.

Where you meet it at KKR

Maintaining CRM data and "assisting with sales territory management" [S107] CONFIRMED; briefing notes that let a new senior pick up a client history in an hour INFERRED.

Say it in one line

"I want to learn how KCS keeps a client relationship continuous across a leadership change, because the record a junior keeps is what makes that possible."

Only at KKR

The Capital Group pairing is KKR's alone: an exclusive partnership in which KKR sub-advises Capital Group-branded funds and a Capital Group equity fund holds a K-Series vehicle [S70, S92] CONFIRMED; other alternatives firms have tie-ups with traditional managers, so the concept is not unique, only the pairing INFERRED. Add Arctos's sports and GP-solutions shelf [S330] and a London desk that sees UK, European and Gulf wealth partners in one year [S177, S367], and the distribution lessons are unusually varied INFERRED.

The rules changing the market@@l6-rules@@

Story 8. The UK Pension Schemes Act 2026 and the Mansion House Accord

What happened. Royal Assent on 29 April 2026 [S346] CONFIRMED. Multi-employer DC schemes need a £25bn default fund by 2030; a reserve power from 1 January 2028 can require up to 10% in qualifying private assets, no more than 5% UK-specific, with a best-interests opt-out [S346] CONFIRMED. The Local Government Pension Scheme (over £400bn, 86 funds in England and Wales) is consolidating from eight pools to six after a missed 31 March 2026 deadline [S347] REPORTED. An Isio study of 13 master trusts found illiquid allocations "rapidly increasing" [S348] REPORTED.

What you will learn

Constraint arithmetic for a pension portfolio. For a £25bn default fund: 10% cap = £2.5bn private; 5% UK limit = at most £1.25bn of that tied to UK-specific assets (the Accord separately pledges at least 5% UK) [S346, S315] CONFIRMED. You also learn why buyer concentration changes product design: six LGPS pools and a few megafunds negotiate like sovereigns, so SMAs and fee breaks follow INFERRED.

Where you meet it at KKR

Alice Bush covers "Family Capital clients across EMEA and UK institutions" [S120] CONFIRMED; Daniel Valladares leads EMEA consultant relations, the gatekeepers to UK schemes [S132] CONFIRMED. UK assets KKR already owns, such as the Norfolk Vanguard wind partnership [S181], become part of the story INFERRED.

Say it in one line

"I want to learn how KCS would build a 10% private markets sleeve for a £25bn UK DC megafund, including what counts toward the 5% UK share."

Story 9. AIFMD II goes live; the FCA proposes a new UK regime

What happened. AIFMD II applied from 16 April 2026: loan-originating funds face leverage caps of 175% (open-ended) and 300% (closed-ended), must keep 5% of loans they sell, and may not originate only to distribute; open-ended funds must pick at least two liquidity tools; delegation needs at least two EU-resident full-time people; new reporting starts 16 April 2027 [S344] CONFIRMED. On 14 July 2026 the FCA published CP26/28: UK managers tiered by net assets (under £750m, £750m to £5bn, over £5bn), lighter disclosures, consultation closing 14 October 2026, rules expected 2027 and in force 2028 [S345] CONFIRMED. UK managers marketing UK funds would move from 20-day pre-approval to notification [S303] CONFIRMED.

What you will learn

The regulatory perimeter. Who is the regulated manager, and which route reaches which investor. KKR's EU manager is authorised in Ireland, and outside the US it uses private placement and marketing registrations [S70] CONFIRMED. Worked (illustrative): an open-ended EU loan fund with €500m NAV may run exposure up to 1.75 × 500 = €875m under a 175% cap INFERRED; selling a €100m loan means keeping €5m [S344].

Where you meet it at KKR

Why K-PRIME is a Luxembourg vehicle for one client and why another is reached by private placement [S111, S70]; the Dublin credit strategy team that structures European credit products [S152] CONFIRMED; compliance sign-off on every pitch INFERRED.

Say it in one line

"I want to learn the plumbing of distribution: why a fund is Luxembourg for one client and private placement for another, and how AIFMD II changed what a credit fund may do."

Story 10. US 401(k): the executive order, the DOL rule and the SEC

What happened. Executive Order 14330, "Democratizing Access to Alternative Assets for 401(k) Investors", was signed on 7 August 2025 [S273] CONFIRMED. On 30 March 2026 the Department of Labor proposed a process-based safe harbour: plan fiduciaries who weigh six factors (performance, fees, liquidity, valuation, benchmarks, complexity) get legal protection [S342] CONFIRMED. Comments closed on 1 June 2026, and no final rule was found by 11 October [S342, S343] REPORTED. Around 30 September 2026 the SEC proposed new accredited investor routes, including an exam and CFA, CPA and CFP credentials [S275] REPORTED.

What you will learn

Fiduciary process as a product requirement. The six DOL factors are a checklist a manager must help a plan sponsor complete, much like a due diligence questionnaire INFERRED. A product that cannot document valuation and liquidity cannot enter the channel. The same six questions are what UK DC trustees ask INFERRED.

Where you meet it at KKR

The Capital Group target-date fund plan [S363] REPORTED; Daniel Celeghin's defined contribution remit [S136] CONFIRMED. From London, the transferable lesson is the evidence pack that any DC buyer needs INFERRED.

Say it in one line

"I want to learn how a fund proves itself on fees, liquidity and valuation to a pension fiduciary, because the US 401(k) rule and the UK Pension Schemes Act are asking the same question."

Only at KKR

A London KCS team meets three rulebooks at once: UK pension reform, EU fund law through an Irish manager, and Gulf distribution through KKR MENA and KKR Saudi entities [S70, S151] CONFIRMED. Add US retail rules via the Capital Group partnership [S92], and a junior sees four regulatory regimes shaping one product shelf INFERRED.

The firm itself, and the house view@@l6-firm@@

Story 11. The $250m DOJ settlement and the Sunset Date

What happened. On 26 August 2026 a KKR subsidiary agreed to pay $250m to settle a DOJ civil case over pre-merger (HSR) filings for 2021 and 2022 deals; KKR said the penalty "will have no financial impact on the firm, our funds, or any of our investors and will be fully reimbursed by outside law firms", and that it strongly disagrees with the Antitrust Division's characterisation; the judgment needs court approval [S349] CONFIRMED. Separately, by 31 December 2026 KKR will "cancel the Series I preferred stock, establish voting rights for all common stock on a one vote per share basis, and acquire control of the carry pool" [S332] CONFIRMED. A charter proposal won 86.60% of outstanding shares but needed 90% and was not adopted [S351] CONFIRMED.

What you will learn

How governance and litigation enter fund diligence. The ILPA DDQ 2.0 added sections such as succession planning [S320] REPORTED. A client's question on the DOJ matter is answered with the approved public statement, word for word, never paraphrased INFERRED. The quorum arithmetic: 86.60% < 90%, so a proposal with 97.83% of votes cast still failed [S203] CONFIRMED.

Where you meet it at KKR

Coordinating "responses to client due diligence requests/RFPs" [S141] CONFIRMED with Legal and Compliance, two of the eight internal teams the London analyst works with [S101] CONFIRMED.

Say it in one line

"I want to learn how KCS answers the hard diligence questions, on governance and litigation, accurately and consistently across hundreds of clients."

Story 12. The house view: "High Grading" and "The Divergence Conundrum"

What happened. KKR's "Outlook for 2026: High Grading" (17 December 2025) argued for upgrading quality while it is cheap: "we think a B is an A in this environment, but a C could be an F", and "We want to own more Asia at this point in the cycle" [S361] CONFIRMED. The Mid-Year Outlook "The Divergence Conundrum" (June 2026, Henry McVey and Lauren Goodwin) said "the cycle continues, but the rules of the game are changing", called "Security of Everything" a capital allocation decision, favoured collateral-based cash flows and warned that stocks and bonds diversify each other less [S360] CONFIRMED.

House view callKKR product it points to
"Security of Everything": energy, data, defence, grids [S360]GII V; Helix Digital Infrastructure [S334, S3] CONFIRMED
Collateral-based cash flows [S360, S361]Asset-based finance, $91bn of KKR AUM [S332] CONFIRMED
"Own more Asia" [S361]Asian Fund V, raising in Q2 2026 [S332] CONFIRMED
Stocks and bonds diversify less; add "differentiated return streams" [S360]Total Portfolio Approach conversations [S90] CONFIRMED
What you will learn

Macro to portfolio to product. How a view on inflation's "resting heart rate" or stock-bond correlation becomes an allocation argument, then a specific fund [S360] CONFIRMED; the mapping to products is INFERRED. This is the skill behind the Total Portfolio Approach, which treats the whole portfolio rather than "static asset class silos" [S90] CONFIRMED.

Where you meet it at KKR

Client meetings and quarterly updates that open with the macro slides; the Chief Investment Strategist for Global Wealth (Lauren Goodwin) co-wrote the mid-year note [S360, S138] CONFIRMED.

Say it in one line

"I want to learn how KKR turns a house view like 'Security of Everything' into a portfolio a client can act on, and which fund on the shelf carries it."

Only at KKR

KCS works through a governance change most listed peers made earlier or never needed: founder control ends at the Sunset Date, while employees own about 30% of the stock [S201] CONFIRMED. And the macro team that writes the house view also co-writes for wealth clients [S360], so a London junior can trace a single idea from outlook to product to adviser slide INFERRED.

Using the news in a 1,000-character answer@@l6-use@@

The application question allows about 1,000 characters, so pick one story per skill, not a list. Three pairings work well because each is sourced, recent and technical INFERRED:

  • Liquidity: K-FIT's 6.3% requests against a 5% cap, about 80% paid [S338].
  • Packaging: GII V at $19.2bn beside K-Series Infrastructure and Helix in one quarter [S334, S74].
  • Rules: the Pension Schemes Act's 10% and 5% levels for UK DC megafunds [S346].
Say it in one line

"I hope to learn how KCS matches clients to products when liquidity, rules and capacity all bind at once: why K-FIT pro-rated at 80%, how GII V and K-Series Infrastructure package one strategy twice, and how a UK DC megafund reaches 10% private markets."

@@l7@@
Part 7

What only KKR teaches, and how to say it

The uniqueness test, an eight-week learning map, and the follow-up questions your answer will trigger.

The uniqueness test@@l7-test@@

A claim about learning is only strong if the reader cannot say "you could learn that anywhere". The table applies that test to eleven technical things. The last column is the honest caveat: where a rival offers something close, it says so, so you never overclaim.

You would learnWhere it lives at KKRWhy it is distinctive hereHonest caveat
How one deal is shared between an institutional flagship and a wealth vehicleK-PEC and K-INFRA invest through joint ventures alongside KKR flagship funds, with flagship minimums filled first and K-PEC getting no priority [S420, S421].The allocation rule is written into the filings, so you learn the conflict and how it is disclosed, not just the sales story INFERRED.Blackstone and Apollo run wealth vehicles too, with different legal routes [S420] REPORTED.
Why an evergreen PE fund is legally "not a fund"K-PEC's co-GP joint-venture structure keeps it under the Investment Company Act 40% test [S420].A structural trick few people outside product teams can explain in a sentence INFERRED.The rule is US law; Europe uses K-PRIME, a Luxembourg SICAV [S111].
Evergreen fee and liquidity maths in exact numbers1.25% fee; 15% (PE) or 12.5% (infra) over a 5% hurdle with high-water mark and full catch-up; 5% quarterly repurchase cap; 5% early repurchase fee within 24 months [S420, S421].You compute a real year's performance allocation and model a real queue, rather than textbook "2 and 20" INFERRED.Similar structures exist across the industry; the numbers are KKR's.
One strategy, three price listsInfrastructure as GII V (drawdown), the Diversified Core Infrastructure Fund (0.6% to 0.8% fee, six-year lock, 2021 terms) and K-INFRA [S430, S432, S421].The same team's assets sold to three client types on three sets of terms: product design in plain view INFERRED.DCIF terms come from a 2021 consultant memo and may have changed [S432].
Co-investment economicsFund-level deal fees are fully offset to LPs, but KCM's capital markets fees (about $930m in 2025) are not [S425].KCS, KCM and deal teams meet on one transaction; you see why co-invest matters to both the client and the firm INFERRED.Blackstone and Apollo have capital markets arms; KKR writes the link into KCS's mandate [Advert] [S214, S220].
A retail product built from KKR partsCapital Group KKR U.S. Equity+ holds about 30% in K-PEC and 10% in KKR co-investments; Core Plus+ is 60/40 public/private credit [S427, S426].An exclusive pairing with a large traditional manager [S210] CONFIRMED; you see distribution partnerships from the inside INFERRED.Rivals' tie-ups with traditional managers were not verified in this research.
Insurance capital mathsGlobal Atlantic and the Ivy vehicles ($58bn at end-2025) [S440]; NAIC capital factors on CLOs and collateral loans [S439].An owned insurer is both a client and a source of capital, so KCS insurance work is about capital efficiency, not just return INFERRED.Apollo/Athene is larger in insurance [S220].
Selling a portfolio, not a fundKKR's Total Portfolio Approach papers (Feb and Jul 2026) [S435, S436].The house framework gives KCS a way to move from "buy Fund X" to "build your programme" INFERRED.TPA is an industry concept; KKR's papers are its own version.
The LP's checklist from the manager's sideILPA DDQ 2.0's 20 sections, including co-investment questions; the 2025 ILPA performance template; the CAPS team for tenders [S444, S445, S106].A breadth of strategies means DDQs across PE, infra, credit and real estate in one seat INFERRED.Every large GP answers DDQs.
European liquidity rules as product constraintsAIFMD II's liquidity management tools apply to open-ended funds such as K-PRIME [S449]; Consumer Duty for UK wealth [S308].A London intern sees EU and UK rules shape a live product INFERRED.Tool choices for K-PRIME are not public; ask, do not assert.
Trust as a professional discipline"Like and trust" and Walter Industries [S384]; KKR's culture text: "presenting numbers accurately" [Advert].KKR states it as the hiring test [S385] CONFIRMED.Every firm values integrity; the evidence (17 years on one bankruptcy) is KKR's own.
Don't say this

"I could only learn this at KKR." Say instead "KKR is where I would see this most clearly, because..." and name the mechanism. The difference is one clause and it removes the overclaim.

An eight-week learning map (illustrative)@@l7-weeks@@

Nobody publishes a KCS intern timetable, so this is a reasoned sketch built from KKR's London analyst posting [S101, S140], its Dublin posting on tenders and DDQs [S106] and the modules in this guide INFERRED. Its value is to show you know how learning compounds in a client seat: first the product, then the client, then the judgement.

The follow-up questions your answer will trigger@@l7-follow@@

A specific answer invites specific questions. That is the point: it moves the interview onto ground you have prepared. For each phrase in Version A, here is the likely follow-up and a scaffold for the reply.

"You mentioned K-INFRA. What is it, in one breath?"
Tests: whether the name is decoration.

"KKR's evergreen infrastructure vehicle for individual investors. It owns stakes in KKR infrastructure assets through joint ventures with funds like GII V and the core infrastructure fund, strikes a NAV each month, charges 1.25% plus 12.5% of returns over a 5% hurdle, and lets investors redeem up to 5% of NAV a quarter." [S421]

"Why not just put private bank clients into GII V?"
Tests: whether you understand why wrappers exist.

Minimums, capital calls over years and no liquidity suit a pension, not an individual. The evergreen wrapper invests money at once, offers limited liquidity and accepts small cheques, in exchange for a liquidity sleeve, a gate and a different fee structure [S421] INFERRED. Then add the cost: the 5% gate means a client may not get out when they want, as K-FIT's Q1 2026 queue showed [S338].

"What is the Total Portfolio Approach?"
Tests: whether you read KKR's own research.

Instead of fixed buckets ("10% private equity"), the client sets one return and risk target for the whole portfolio and lets every asset compete for a place against a simple reference portfolio. KKR's 2026 papers describe ideas such as "zero-based budgeting" and a "minimum viable TPA" [S435, S436]. For KCS it turns a fund sale into a portfolio conversation INFERRED.

"Why does co-investment matter to KKR, not just to clients?"
Tests: commercial understanding.

Clients get extra exposure at low or no fee. KKR gets bigger deals done, and capital markets fees earned by KCM are not offset against fund fees, unlike fund-level transaction fees: about $930m of KCM fees in 2025 [S425]. Then add the safeguard: allocation has to be fair and disclosed, which is why ILPA's DDQ asks about it [S444].

"What do you mean by information walls?"
Tests: maturity about rules.

Capital markets work can involve material non-public information about listed companies. People who hold it are separated from people who do not, and anyone brought over the wall is logged and restricted. In a client seat that means never passing on deal information outside a controlled process [Inferred; rules in Part 5].

"Isn't 'trust is the product' a cliché?"
Tests: whether you can back a value with evidence.

"It would be without the evidence. Kravis describes spending almost 17 years on Walter Industries to get fund investors their money back [S384]. In a client team the same idea is mechanical: if one number in a DDQ is wrong, every other number becomes doubtful."

The one-paragraph version for an interview@@l7-close@@

Say it in one line (or four)

"I want to learn the technical core of a client seat at a firm built as one P&L. That means three things: the plumbing of how one strategy is packaged for different clients, like infrastructure through GII V or K-INFRA; how clients build portfolios, from pacing to KKR's Total Portfolio Approach; and how co-investment flows from Capital Markets to clients behind proper walls. KKR is where I would see all three in one seat."

@@l8@@
Part 8

Glossary

Every term in plain English.

How to use the glossary@@g-how@@

Terms are grouped by theme so you can learn them in clusters, not alphabetically. Bold words inside a definition are themselves defined somewhere on these pages. KKR-specific names carry a source marker; general finance definitions are standard usage.

1. The basic cast

TermPlain-English meaning
Alternative asset managerA firm that invests other people's money in things outside listed shares and bonds: private companies, loans, buildings, infrastructure. KKR, Blackstone and Apollo are the largest.
Traditional asset managerA firm that mainly buys listed shares and bonds, often cheaply in index funds (BlackRock, Vanguard). Lower fees, daily liquidity.
General partner (GP)The manager that runs a private fund, picks the investments and earns fees and carried interest. KKR is the GP of its funds.
Limited partner (LP)An investor in a private fund: a pension, insurer, sovereign wealth fund, endowment, family office or wealthy individual. "Limited" because they can lose only what they put in and do not run the fund.
Client / investorKKR's own word for LPs and other buyers of its products. KCS calls them clients.
Portfolio companyA company a private equity fund owns.
SponsorA private equity firm in its role as owner and backer of a deal. "Sponsor-backed" means owned by a PE firm.
Buy side / sell sideBuy side invests money (KKR, pensions). Sell side sells services to investors (investment banks, brokers).
Family officeA private company that manages one wealthy family's money. KKR calls this channel Family Capital [S84].
Sovereign wealth fund (SWF)A state-owned investment fund, for example in Norway, Abu Dhabi, Saudi Arabia or Singapore.
EndowmentThe investment pot of a university or charity, spent slowly over very long horizons.
Defined benefit (DB) pensionA pension that promises a set income in retirement. The scheme carries the investment risk, so it invests to meet future payments (liabilities).
Defined contribution (DC) pensionA pension where each saver has a pot and carries the investment risk themselves. Workplace DC pots are the target of the UK Mansion House reforms.
Private wealthWealthy individuals investing through private banks, wealth managers and financial advisers. Smaller cheques, many more investors, stricter consumer rules.
WirehouseA large US brokerage with thousands of financial advisers (for example Morgan Stanley, Merrill). A key gateway to private wealth.
Insurer / annuityAn insurer collects premiums and invests them to pay future claims. An annuity pays a retiree a fixed income for life. Long-dated promises suit long-dated private assets.

2. How a private fund works

TermPlain-English meaning
Closed-end / drawdown fundA fund with a fixed size and life (often 10-12 years). Investors promise money up front and hand it over only when the GP asks.
CommitmentThe amount an LP promises to a drawdown fund. Not paid on day one.
Capital call (drawdown)The GP's request for part of the commitment, usually with 10 business days' notice, when it needs cash for a deal.
Dry powderMoney committed to funds but not yet invested.
First close / final closeThe first date the fund can start investing with commitments so far, and the last date new investors can join.
Hard capThe maximum size the GP has promised the fund will reach.
Investment periodUsually the first 4-6 years, when the fund buys new assets.
Harvest periodThe later years, when the fund sells assets and returns cash.
DistributionCash paid back to LPs after a sale or from income.
ExitSelling an investment: to another company (trade sale), to another PE firm (secondary buyout), or by listing it (IPO).
Flagship fundA firm's main, numbered fund in a strategy, e.g. KKR North America Fund XIV, European Fund VI, Global Infrastructure Investors V [S2].
Vintage yearThe year a fund starts investing. Used to compare funds fairly.
J-curveThe shape of an LP's returns over time: negative early (fees, no sales yet), positive later as investments are sold.
Re-upAn existing LP committing again to the next fund in the series. A core KCS success measure.
Evergreen / semi-liquid fundA fund with no end date that takes new money continuously and lets investors redeem, usually monthly or quarterly, up to a limit. The main product for private wealth. KKR's are the K-Series.
NAV (net asset value)The value of a fund's assets minus its debts. Evergreen funds issue and redeem units at NAV.
Redemption gateA cap on how much investors can withdraw in a period (often 5% of NAV a quarter). Protects remaining investors from fire sales.
Interval fundA US registered fund that offers to buy back a set percentage of shares at set intervals. The Capital Group KKR funds use this structure [S92].
BDC (business development company)A US vehicle that lends to mid-sized companies and can be sold to retail investors. K-FIT is one [S113].
Non-traded REITA real estate investment trust that is not listed on an exchange. Sold to wealth clients; KREST is KKR's [S114].
Feeder fundA vehicle that pools many small investors and invests as one LP into the main fund. Platforms like iCapital run them.
Separately managed account (SMA)A portfolio run for one large client under its own rules, instead of a pooled fund. One meaning of "customised products".
Co-investmentAn LP investing directly in a specific deal alongside the fund, usually with low or no fees. Highly valued by LPs; at KKR, syndicated with KCM [S70].
SyndicationSelling pieces of a deal or a loan the firm has committed to, to other investors.
SecondariesBuying or selling existing fund stakes or assets from other investors, often at a discount, to give early liquidity.
Continuation vehicle (CV)A new fund a GP sets up to buy one or more assets from its own older fund, so existing LPs can cash out or roll over.
Strategic partnershipA large multi-strategy mandate with one client across several KKR products, often with fee terms and co-investment rights.

3. Fees and returns

TermPlain-English meaning
Management feeAn annual fee on committed or invested capital (traditionally about 1.5-2% for buyout funds). Pays for the team whatever happens.
Carried interest (carry)The GP's share of profits (traditionally 20%) once LPs have their money back plus the hurdle.
Hurdle / preferred returnThe minimum annual return LPs get before carry starts, often 8%.
Catch-upAfter the hurdle, the GP takes a larger share for a while until it has "caught up" to its full 20%.
WaterfallThe order in which cash is split between LPs and GP: capital back, hurdle, catch-up, then 80/20.
ClawbackThe GP returns carry it was paid early if the fund later underperforms. KKR repaid $344m on Asian Fund II in Q4 2025 [S2].
GP commitmentThe GP's own money invested in its fund. "Skin in the game".
Side letterA private agreement giving one LP special terms (fees, reporting, co-invest rights).
MFN (most favoured nation)A clause letting an LP claim terms given to other similar-sized LPs.
Basis point (bp)One hundredth of a percent. 84bp = 0.84%.
IRR (internal rate of return)The annualised return that accounts for the timing of cash in and out. Sensitive to timing; can flatter quick wins.
MOIC / TVPIMultiple on invested capital / total value to paid-in: total value (cash returned plus value still held) divided by money put in. 2.0x = doubled.
DPI (distributions to paid-in)Cash actually returned divided by money paid in. The "real money" metric; the word of 2025-26 because exits have been slow.
RVPIResidual value to paid-in: the unsold part. TVPI = DPI + RVPI.
PME (public market equivalent)Compares a fund's return with what the same cash flows would have earned in a stock index.
Sharpe ratioReturn per unit of volatility. Higher means a smoother ride for the return earned.
Denominator effectWhen public markets fall, private holdings become a bigger share of an LP's portfolio, pushing it over its target and making it pause new commitments.

4. KKR's own vocabulary

TermPlain-English meaning
KCS (Global Client Solutions)KKR's fundraising and client team, formed in 2009, about 300+ people, split into sales/relationship management and product/strategy management [S103].
KCM (KKR Capital Markets)KKR's in-house capital markets business: arranges debt and equity financing for KKR deals and portfolio companies and syndicates co-investment.
CapstoneKKR's in-house team of operating specialists, mostly ex-consultants, who work inside portfolio companies on operational improvements.
Global AtlanticKKR's insurance subsidiary (retirement, life and reinsurance). Its policyholder money is a large, long-term source of capital for KKR's credit strategies.
Strategic HoldingsKKR's segment of long-term stakes in businesses held on its own balance sheet.
AUM / FPAUMAssets under management / fee-paying AUM, the part that earns management fees. KKR: $796.5bn AUM and $638.4bn FPAUM at 30 Jun 2026 [S1].
FRE (fee related earnings)Profit from recurring fees after paying the people and costs that earn them. Investors prize it for stability.
ANI (adjusted net income)KKR's headline profit measure including carry, insurance and balance sheet income.
K-SeriesKKR's evergreen funds for private wealth: K-PEC/K-PRIME (private equity), K-INFRA (infrastructure), K-FIT/K-FITS (private credit), KREST (real estate) [S111-S114].
Family CapitalKKR's client segment for family offices, offering co-investment, customised portfolios, SMAs, two-way sourcing and debt solutions [S84].
CAPS teamThe internal KKR team that completes tenders and due diligence questionnaires with KCS [S106].
Sunset DateThe date, no later than 31 Dec 2026, when the founders' special control ends and every KKR share gets one vote.
Investment Committee (IC) memoThe document a deal team writes to ask senior partners to approve an investment.

5. Fundraising and client work

TermPlain-English meaning
Investor relations (IR)Keeping existing investors informed and happy. At KKR, part of KCS's "servicing" job.
Capital formationIndustry word for raising money: new clients, new products, new channels.
Relationship manager (coverage)The person who "owns" a set of clients, usually by region or investor type.
Product specialistThe expert on one strategy (say infrastructure) who explains it in depth to clients.
Pitch bookThe presentation used to market a fund.
PPM (private placement memorandum)The formal offering document for a private fund.
LPA (limited partnership agreement)The legal contract governing the fund.
Data roomA secure online folder of fund documents for investors doing diligence.
DDQ (due diligence questionnaire)A long standard questionnaire investors send a manager. ILPA publishes a template.
RFP / tenderRequest for proposal: a formal competition where an investor invites managers to bid for a mandate.
ODD (operational due diligence)Checking the manager's operations, controls, valuation and compliance, separate from the investment case.
Investment consultant / gatekeeperAdvisers such as Cambridge Associates, Mercer or Hamilton Lane who recommend managers to pensions and others.
Placement agentAn outside firm paid to help a GP raise a fund (for example Evercore, Campbell Lutyens, PJT Park Hill).
ILPAInstitutional Limited Partners Association, the LP trade body that publishes best-practice principles and reporting templates.
AGM / annual meetingThe yearly meeting where a GP updates its LPs on a fund.
CRMClient relationship management software. KKR postings name Salesforce [S103].
Cross-sellGetting a client who invests in one strategy to invest in another.

6. Asset classes and deal words

TermPlain-English meaning
Private equity (PE)Buying stakes in private companies, usually control, improving them and selling within years.
Leveraged buyout (LBO)Buying a company using a lot of borrowed money secured on the company itself. KKR's founding idea.
Growth equityMinority stakes in fast-growing companies that need capital to expand.
Impact investingInvesting for a financial return and a measurable social or environmental result. KKR Global Impact focuses on climate, learning, sustainable living and inclusive growth.
Private creditLoans made by non-bank funds directly to companies or backed by assets. The fastest-growing private asset class.
Direct lendingPrivate credit lent straight to mid-sized companies, mostly floating rate.
Asset-based finance (ABF)Lending secured on pools of assets (loans, equipment, receivables) rather than a company's cash flow.
InfrastructureLong-life physical assets providing essential services: power, data centres, fibre, transport, utilities.
Core / core-plus / value-add / opportunisticThe risk ladder for real assets, from stable income (core) to heavy repositioning (opportunistic).
Real estateBuildings and land: logistics, housing, offices, data centres. Earns rent and value growth.
ECM / DCMEquity capital markets (share issues, IPOs) and debt capital markets (bonds, loans).
Due diligenceThe deep investigation before an investment: financial, commercial, legal, operational.
Value creationHow an owner makes a company worth more: growth, margins, better capital structure, bolt-on acquisitions.
Investment theme / thesisA view about a long trend (say, power demand from data centres) used to find deals.

7. Rules and regulators

TermPlain-English meaning
FCAFinancial Conduct Authority, the UK conduct regulator. KKR's UK LLP is FCA-authorised (FRN 593180) [S23].
SECUS Securities and Exchange Commission, the US securities regulator.
AIF / AIFMAlternative investment fund / its manager. Almost every private fund is an AIF.
AIFMD / AIFMD IIThe EU law on alternative fund managers (2011) and its revision, which member states had to transpose by 16 April 2026.
Marketing passportThe EU right for an authorised EU manager to market a fund to professional investors across the EU.
NPPRNational private placement regime: country-by-country rules for marketing a fund without a passport (the UK route for non-UK funds).
Reverse solicitationAn investor approaching a manager on its own initiative, which can fall outside marketing rules. Narrowly interpreted; never rely on it casually.
Pre-marketingTesting investor appetite before a fund is formally launched, under strict EU conditions.
ELTIF 2.0The revamped EU long-term investment fund regime (applies from 10 Jan 2024) that lets retail investors buy private-market funds.
LTAFThe UK long-term asset fund (2021), an open-ended fund structure for private assets aimed at DC pensions and, later, wider investors.
Financial promotionAny UK communication inviting someone to invest. Must be fair, clear and not misleading, and approved by an authorised person.
Consumer DutyThe FCA's rule (from July 2023) that firms must deliver good outcomes for retail customers.
Professional / retail clientThe regulatory split: professionals (institutions, some wealthy individuals) can be offered more complex products than retail clients.
Accredited investorThe US test (income or wealth) for who may buy most private funds.
Marketing RuleThe SEC's rule on adviser advertising, including how performance can be shown.
MNPI / inside informationMaterial non-public information about a listed company. Trading on it or passing it on is illegal.
Information barrier ("Chinese wall")Policies separating people who hold MNPI (often capital markets) from others.
SMCRThe UK Senior Managers and Certification Regime, which makes named individuals accountable for conduct.
Mansion House AccordA May 2025 voluntary pledge by UK pension providers to put more of their default DC funds into private markets.
Pension Schemes Act 2026UK law (Royal Assent 29 Apr 2026) consolidating DC schemes into large funds and allowing government to set private asset allocations.
